K\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq K\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{P}\|_{\mathrm{op}}+K\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{P}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathcal{P}}(1),

and by the second part of Proposition
S23
(d),

K^{-1}\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq K^{-1}\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}^{-1}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{P}^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}+K^{-1}\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{P}^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathcal{P}}(1),

as required.
∎

Proposition
S29
below provides estimation and both in-sample and out-of-sample prediction bounds for spline regression. It is based on
Belloni et al., 2015
, but here we control the errors in a uniform fashion over a family of distributions, and those authors did not require in-sample bounds.

Proposition S29
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a family of distributions of
(Y,Z)
on
\mathbb{R}\times[0,1]^{d}
with regression function
f_{P}
given by
f_{P}(z):={\mathbb{E}}_{P}(Y\,|\,Z=z)
, and let
(Y_{1},Z_{1}),\dots,(Y_{n},Z_{n})
be independent and identically distributed copies of
(Y,Z)
. Suppose that

(i)

The
L_{2}(P)
-best approximant
f_{P}^{\dagger}
of
f_{P}
in
\mathcal{S}_{r,N}^{d}
satisfies

\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\|f_{P}-f_{P}^{\dagger}\|_{\infty}=O(K^{-\zeta}),

for some
\zeta\equiv\zeta(d,r)>0
.

(ii)

Each
P\in\mathcal{P}
is absolutely continuous with respect to Lebesgue measure on
[0,1]^{d}
, with corresponding density
p_{P}
satisfying
C:=\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\sup_{z\in[0,1]^{d}}p_{P}(z)<\infty
and
c:=\inf_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\inf_{z\in[0,1]^{d}}p_{P}(z)>0
.

(iii)

There exists a positive sequence
(\sigma_{n}^{2})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
such that
{\mathrm{Var}}_{P}(Y\,|\,Z)\leq\sigma_{n}^{2}
for all
P\in\mathcal{P}
.

Let
\boldsymbol{\phi}
denote the
d
-tensor B-spline basis of
\mathcal{S}^{d}_{r,N}
and let
\boldsymbol{\widehat{\beta}}
denote the ordinary least squares estimate from regressing
Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{n}
onto
\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{1}),\ldots,\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{n})
. Assume that
K\log(K)/n\to 0
. Then

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigl(f_{P}(Z_{i})-\boldsymbol{\widehat{\beta}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{i})\bigr)^{2}=O_{\mathcal{P}}\bigl(K^{-2\zeta}+\sigma_{n}^{2}K/n\bigr).

Letting
\boldsymbol{\beta}_{P}\in\mathbb{R}^{K}
be the unique solution to
f_{P}^{\dagger}(z)=\boldsymbol{\beta}_{P}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\phi}(z)
, we have under the same assumptions that

\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{P}\|_{2}^{2}=O_{\mathcal{P}}\bigl(K^{-(2\zeta-2)}/n+\sigma_{n}^{2}K^{2}/n\bigr).

Further, if
(Y^{*},Z^{*})
is a new observation of
(Y,Z)
independent of the original sample, then

{\mathbb{E}}_{P}\Bigl(\bigl\{f_{P}(Z^{*})-\boldsymbol{\widehat{\beta}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z^{*})\bigr\}^{2}\bigm|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\Bigr)=O_{\mathcal{P}}\bigl(K^{-2\zeta}+\sigma_{n}^{2}K/n\bigr).

Proof.

Let
\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{i})\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{i})^{\top}
and for
i\in[n]
, let
h_{i}:=f_{P}(Z_{i})-f_{P}^{\dagger}(Z_{i})
and
\varepsilon_{i}:=Y_{i}-f_{P}(Z_{i})
. Then, recalling that
f_{P}^{\dagger}(z)=\boldsymbol{\beta}_{P}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\phi}(z)
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigl(f_{P}(Z_{i})-\boldsymbol{\widehat{\beta}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{i})\bigr)^{2}\leq 2\|f_{P}-f_{P}^{\dagger}\|_{\infty}^{2}+2(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{P})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{P})

\displaystyle=2\|f_{P}-f_{P}^{\dagger}\|_{\infty}^{2}+2\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(h_{i}+\varepsilon_{i})\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{i})\biggr)^{\top}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}^{-1}\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(h_{i}+\varepsilon_{i})\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{i})\biggr)

\displaystyle\leq 2\|f_{P}-f_{P}^{\dagger}\|_{\infty}^{2}+2\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\bigl\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}^{-1}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}\biggl\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}^{-1/2}\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(h_{i}+\varepsilon_{i})\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{i})\biggr)\biggr\|_{2}^{2}.

(S46)

Now
\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\bigl\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}^{-1}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathcal{P}}(1)
by (
S45
) in Lemma
S28
. Moreover,

\displaystyle\biggl\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}^{-1/2}\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(h_{i}+\varepsilon_{i})\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{i})\biggr)\biggr\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\underbrace{\biggl\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}^{-1/2}\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{i}\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{i})\biggr)\biggr\|_{2}^{2}}_{\textup{I}_{n}}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+2\underbrace{\biggl\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}^{-1/2}\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{i}\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{i})\biggr)\biggr\|_{2}^{2}}_{\textup{II}_{n}}.

To deal with
\textup{I}_{n}
, let
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{P}:={\mathbb{E}}_{P}\bigl(\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z)\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z)^{\top}\bigr)\in\mathbb{R}^{K\times K}
, and note that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{P}\bigl(f_{P}^{\dagger}(Z)\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z)^{\top}\bigr)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{P}\bigl(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{P}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z)\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z)^{\top}\bigr)

(S47)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{P}\bigl(f_{P}(Z)\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z)^{\top}\bigr)\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1}_{P}\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigl(\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z)\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z)^{\top}\bigr)={\mathbb{E}}_{P}\bigl(f_{P}(Z)\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z)^{\top}\bigr).

It follows that

\displaystyle\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}{\mathbb{E}}_{P}\biggl(\biggl\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{i}\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{i})\biggr\|_{2}^{2}\biggr)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}{\mathbb{E}}_{P}\bigl\{{\mathrm{tr}}\bigl((h_{1})^{2}\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{1})\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{1})^{\top}\bigr)\bigr\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\|f_{P}-f_{P}^{\dagger}\|_{\infty}^{2}{\mathrm{tr}}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{P}),

(S48)

so

|\textup{I}_{n}|\leq\bigl\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}^{-1}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}\biggl\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{i}\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{i})\biggr\|_{2}^{2}=O_{\mathcal{P}}\bigl(K^{-(2\zeta-1)}/n\bigr)

(S49)

by our assumption on
\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\|f_{P}-f_{P}^{\dagger}\|_{\infty}
, Proposition
S23
(d), (
S45
) in Lemma
S28
and Lemma
S2
.
To deal with
\textup{II}_{n}
, we note that
\varepsilon_{1},\dots,\varepsilon_{n}
are conditionally independent given
Z_{1},\dots,Z_{n}
, so

{\mathbb{E}}_{P}(\textup{II}_{n}\,|\,Z_{1},\dots,Z_{n})=\frac{1}{n^{2}}{\mathrm{tr}}\biggl(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{E}}(\varepsilon_{i}^{2}\,|\,Z_{i})\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{i})\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{i})^{\top}\biggr)\leq\frac{\sigma_{n}^{2}}{n}{\mathrm{tr}}\bigl(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}\bigr)\leq\frac{\sigma_{n}^{2}K}{n}.

(S50)

Putting things together, since
\|f_{P}-f_{P}^{\dagger}\|_{\infty}^{2}=O(K^{-2\zeta})
by assumption, we have

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigl(f_{P}(Z_{i})-\boldsymbol{\widehat{\beta}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{i})\bigr)^{2}=O_{\mathcal{P}}\bigl(K^{-2\zeta}+\sigma_{n}^{2}K/n\bigr),

as desired.

For the second claim, observe that

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{P}\|_{2}^{2}=\biggl\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}^{-1}\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(h_{i}+\varepsilon_{i})\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{i})\biggr)\biggr\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\bigl\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}^{-1}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}\biggl\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}^{-1/2}\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(h_{i}+\varepsilon_{i})\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{i})\biggr)\biggr\|_{2}^{2}=O_{\mathcal{P}}\bigl(K^{-(2\zeta-2)}/n+\sigma_{n}^{2}K^{2}/n\bigr),

by (
S45
) in Lemma
S28
and our results above.

Finally, we have following the argument in (
S46
), we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{P}\Bigl(\bigl\{f_{P}(Z^{*})-\boldsymbol{\widehat{\beta}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z^{*})\bigr\}^{2}\bigm|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq 2\|f_{P}-f_{P}^{\dagger}\|_{\infty}^{2}+2\bigl\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{P}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}\bigl\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{P}\bigr\|^{2}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathcal{P}}\bigl(K^{-2\zeta}+\sigma_{n}^{2}K/n\bigr),

by Proposition
S23
(d) and the second claim of the proposition.
∎

Under standard smoothness assumptions, we can derive the following consequence of Proposition
S29
:

Corollary S30
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a family of distributions of
(Y,Z)
on
\mathbb{R}\times[0,1]^{d}
, and let
f_{P}
denote the regression function given by
f_{P}(z):={\mathbb{E}}_{P}(Y\,|\,Z=z)
for
P\in\mathcal{P}
. Suppose there exist
C,c,s>0
such that

(i)

f_{P}\in\mathcal{H}_{s}
with
\|f_{P}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{s}}\leq C
for all
P\in\mathcal{P}
.

(ii)

Each
P\in\mathcal{P}
is absolutely continuous with respect to Lebesgue measure on
[0,1]^{d}
, with corresponding density
p_{P}
satisfying

\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\sup_{z\in[0,1]^{d}}p_{P}(z)\leq C\quad\text{and}\quad\inf_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\inf_{z\in[0,1]^{d}}p_{P}(z)\geq c.

(iii)

{\mathrm{Var}}_{P}(Y\,|\,Z)\leq C
for all
P\in\mathcal{P}
.

If
K\log(K)/n\rightarrow 0
, then the conclusions of Proposition
S29
hold for any
r\geq s
with
\zeta=s/d
and
\sigma_{n}^{2}=C
.

Proof.

Under Assumptions
(i)
and
(ii)
of the corollary, we have that Assumption
(i)
of Proposition
S29
holds with
\zeta=s/d
when
r\geq s
by Propositions
S26
and
S27
. Assumptions
(ii)
and
(iii)
of Proposition
S29
also hold by hypothesis with
\sigma_{n}^{2}=C
, so the conclusion follows.
∎

Now suppose that
\boldsymbol{\phi}^{X}
and
\boldsymbol{\phi}^{Z}
are the
d_{X}
- and
d_{Z}
-tensor B-spline bases of
\mathcal{S}_{r,N_{X}}^{d_{X}}
and
\mathcal{S}_{r,N_{Z}}^{d_{Z}}
respectively. It will be convenient to have the following decomposition of functions in the span of
\boldsymbol{\phi}^{X}\otimes\boldsymbol{\phi}^{Z}
.

Proposition S31
.

Let
\boldsymbol{\phi}:=\boldsymbol{\phi}^{X}\otimes\boldsymbol{\phi}^{Z}
, and let
K_{X}:=(N_{X}+r)^{d_{X}}
,
K_{Z}:=(N_{Z}+r)^{d_{Z}}
and
K_{XZ}:=K_{XZ}
. Let
V
denote the subspace of
R^{K_{XZ}}
given by
V:=\{\boldsymbol{1}\otimes\boldsymbol{v}:\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{K_{Z}}\}
where
\boldsymbol{1}
denotes the vector of ones in
\mathbb{R}^{K_{X}}
. Let
V^{\perp}
denote the orthogonal complement of
V
in
\mathbb{R}^{K_{XZ}}
, and let
\boldsymbol{\Pi}:\mathbb{R}^{K_{XZ}}\rightarrow V^{\perp}
denote the projection onto
V^{\perp}
. Let
\boldsymbol{\beta}=(\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{K_{XZ}})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{K_{XZ}}
, and define
f:[0,1]^{d_{X}+d_{Z}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
by
f(x,z):=\boldsymbol{\phi}(x,z)^{\top}\boldsymbol{\beta}
. Then, writing
\bar{\boldsymbol{\beta}}=(\bar{\beta}_{1},\ldots,\bar{\beta}_{K_{Z}})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{K_{Z}}
, where
\bar{\beta}_{k}:=K_{X}^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{K_{X}}\beta_{(k-1)K_{X}+\ell}
, we have
(\boldsymbol{I}-\boldsymbol{\Pi})\boldsymbol{\beta}=\boldsymbol{1}\otimes\bar{\boldsymbol{\beta}}
and

f(x,z)=\boldsymbol{\phi}(x,z)^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Pi}\boldsymbol{\beta}+\boldsymbol{\phi}^{Z}(z)^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{\beta}}.

(S51)

Moreover,
\|\boldsymbol{\Pi}\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{\infty}\leq 2\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{\infty}
.

Proof.

We claim that
V^{\perp}=\bigl\{\boldsymbol{u}=(\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\top},\ldots,\boldsymbol{u}_{K_{Z}}^{\top})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{K_{XZ}}:\boldsymbol{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{u}_{k}=0\ \forall k\in[K_{Z}]\bigr\}
. To see this, note that if

\boldsymbol{u}=(\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\top},\ldots,\boldsymbol{u}_{K_{Z}}^{\top})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{K_{XZ}}
satisfies
\boldsymbol{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{u}_{k}=0
for all
k\in[K_{Z}]
and
\boldsymbol{1}\otimes\boldsymbol{v}\in V
for some
\boldsymbol{v}=(v_{1},\ldots,v_{K_{Z}})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{K_{Z}}
, then

(\boldsymbol{1}\otimes\boldsymbol{v})^{\top}\boldsymbol{u}=\sum_{k=1}^{K_{Z}}v_{k}(\boldsymbol{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{u}_{k})=0,

which establishes our claim. We can therefore write

\boldsymbol{\beta}=\boldsymbol{1}\otimes\bar{\boldsymbol{\beta}}+\boldsymbol{\beta}-(\boldsymbol{1}\otimes\bar{\boldsymbol{\beta}}),

where
\boldsymbol{1}\otimes\bar{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in V
and
\boldsymbol{\beta}-(\boldsymbol{1}\otimes\bar{\boldsymbol{\beta}})\in V^{\perp}
, so
(\boldsymbol{I}-\boldsymbol{\Pi})\boldsymbol{\beta}=\boldsymbol{1}\otimes\bar{\boldsymbol{\beta}}
and
\boldsymbol{\Pi}\boldsymbol{\beta}=\boldsymbol{\beta}-(\boldsymbol{1}\otimes\bar{\boldsymbol{\beta}})
. Hence,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\phi}(x,z)^{\top}(\boldsymbol{I}-\boldsymbol{\Pi})\boldsymbol{\beta}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{vec}\bigl(\boldsymbol{\phi}^{X}(x)\boldsymbol{\phi}^{Z}(z)^{\top}\bigr)^{\top}\mathrm{vec}(\boldsymbol{1}\bar{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\top})=\sum_{\ell=1}^{K_{X}}\sum_{k=1}^{K_{Z}}\boldsymbol{\phi}^{X}_{\ell}(x)\boldsymbol{\phi}^{Z}_{k}(z)\bar{\beta}_{k}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\phi}^{Z}(z)^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{\beta}},

by Proposition
S23
(a), from which (
S51
) follows. Finally,
\|\boldsymbol{\Pi}\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{\infty}\leq\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{\infty}+\|\boldsymbol{1}\otimes\bar{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\|_{\infty}\leq 2\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{\infty}
.
∎

Our next two lemmas will be used in the proof of Proposition
S34
, which is the analogue of Corollary
S30
for a key setting for us, namely where our response variable for spline regression consists of fitted values from an earlier spline regression.

Lemma S32
.

Let
P
denote a distribution of
(X,Z)
on
[0,1]^{d_{X}}\times[0,1]^{d_{Z}}
that is absolutely continuous with respect to Lebesgue measure, and let
p_{X|Z}
denote the conditional density of
X
given
Z
. Assume that
p_{X|Z}(x\,|\,\cdot)\in\mathcal{H}^{d_{Z}}_{s}
for every
x\in[0,1]^{d_{X}}
and that there exists
C>0
such that

\sup_{x\in[0,1]^{d_{X}}}\|p_{X|Z}(x\,|\,\cdot)\|_{\mathcal{H}_{s}}\leq C.

Let
\boldsymbol{\phi}=(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{K})^{\top}
denote the
d_{X}
-tensor B-spline basis of
\mathcal{S}_{r,N}^{d_{X}}
, let
\boldsymbol{\beta}=(\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{K})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{K}
and define
g:[0,1]^{d_{Z}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
by

\displaystyle g(z):=\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}\bigl(\boldsymbol{\phi}(X)\,|\,Z=z\bigr)=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\beta_{k}{\mathbb{E}}\bigl(\phi_{k}(X)\,|\,Z=z\bigr).

Then
g\in\mathcal{H}_{s}^{d_{Z}}
and
\|g\|_{\mathcal{H}_{s}}\leq C\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{\infty}
.

Proof.

Repeated application of
Klenke, 2020
allows us to interchange derivatives and integrals such that for any multi-index
\boldsymbol{\alpha}=(\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{d_{Z}})^{\top}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d_{Z}}
with
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|\leq\left\lceil s\right\rceil-1=:s_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}g(z)=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\beta_{k}\int_{[0,1]^{d_{X}}}\phi_{k}(x)\cdot D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}p_{X|Z}(x\,|\,z)\,\mathrm{d}x.

Thus, by Hölder’s inequality and the fact that the
\{\phi_{k}\}_{k=1}^{K}
are non-negative and form a partition of unity by Proposition
S23
(a), we have

\|D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}g\|_{\infty}\leq\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{\infty}\sup_{z\in[0,1]^{d_{Z}}}\int_{[0,1]^{d_{X}}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\phi_{k}(x)\bigl|D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}p_{X|Z}(x\,|\,z)\bigr|\,\mathrm{d}x\leq C\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{\infty}.

By a similar argument, when
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=s_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle\big|D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}g(z)-D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}g(z^{\prime})\big|

\displaystyle\leq\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{\infty}\int_{[0,1]^{d_{X}}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\phi_{k}(x)\bigl|D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}p_{X|Z}(x\,|\,z)-D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}p_{X|Z}(x\,|\,z^{\prime})\bigr|\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq C\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{\infty}\|z-z^{\prime}\|_{2}^{s-s_{0}},

for all
z,z^{\prime}\in[0,1]^{d_{Z}}
, as required.
∎

Lemma S33
.

Let
\{\phi_{k}\}_{k=1}^{K}
denote the uniform
d
-tensor B-spline basis of
\mathcal{S}^{d}_{r,N}
. For
k\in[K]
, suppose that
h_{k}:[0,1]^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
can be written as
h_{k}=s_{k}+r_{k}
where
s_{k}\in\mathcal{S}^{d}_{r,N}
and
r_{k}\in\mathcal{H}_{s}^{d}
, and let
M:=\max_{k\in[K]}\|r_{k}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{s}}
. Define
m:[0,1]^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
by

m(z):=\sum_{k=1}^{K}g_{k}(z)\phi_{k}(z).

Then there exist
C(d,r)>0
and
m^{*}\in\mathcal{S}_{2r-1,N}^{d}
such that

\|m-m^{*}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{MC(d,r)}{(2rK)^{\min(s,r)/d}}.

Proof.

Let

\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}:=\mathrm{span}\bigl(\{\phi_{k}(z)\phi_{\ell}(z)\}_{k,\ell\in[K]}\bigr)\subseteq\mathcal{S}^{d}_{2r-1,N}.

For
k\in[K]
, let
r_{k}^{*}
denote a supremum norm approximant to
r_{k}
in
\mathcal{S}_{r,N}^{d}
(see Proposition
S26
), so that
m^{*}:=\sum_{k=1}^{K}(s_{k}+r_{k}^{*})\phi_{k}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}
. Then by Hölder’s inequality, Proposition
S23
(a) and Proposition
S26
, we have

\|m-m^{*}\|_{\infty}=\biggl\|\sum_{k=1}^{K}(r_{k}-r_{k}^{*})\phi_{k}\biggr\|_{\infty}\leq\max_{k\in K}\|r_{k}-r_{k}^{*}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{MC(d,r)}{(2rK)^{\min(s,r)/d}},

as desired.
∎

We are now in a position to state our main result on the performance of the spline on spline regression procedure.

Proposition S34
.

Let
r\in\mathbb{N}
, let
d=d_{X}+d_{Z}
and let
\boldsymbol{\phi}
denote the
d
-tensor B-spline basis of
\mathcal{S}_{r,N}^{d}
. Let
\mathcal{P}
be a family of distributions of
(X,Z)
on
[0,1]^{d_{X}}\times[0,1]^{d_{Z}}
. Suppose that each
P\in\mathcal{P}
is absolutely continuous with respect to Lebesgue measure on
[0,1]^{d}
. Further, suppose that there exist
C,c>0
such that:

(i)

There exists
s\in(0,r]
such that the conditional density
p_{X|Z,P}
of
X
given
Z
, satisfies
p_{X|Z,P}(x|\cdot)\in\mathcal{H}_{s}^{d_{Z}}
for every
x\in[0,1]^{d_{X}}
and
\sup_{x\in[0,1]^{d_{X}}}\|p_{X|Z,P}(x\,|\,\cdot)\|_{\mathcal{H}_{s}}\leq C
.

(ii)

For every
P\in\mathcal{P}
, the density
p_{Z,P}
of
Z
satisfies

\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\sup_{z\in[0,1]^{d}}p_{Z,P}(z)\leq C\quad\text{and}\quad\inf_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\inf_{z\in[0,1]^{d}}p_{Z,P}(z)\geq c.

Let
(X_{1},Z_{1}),\dots,(X_{n},Z_{n})
be independent and identically distributed copies of
(X,Z)
. For
n\in\mathbb{N}
, let
\boldsymbol{\beta}\equiv\boldsymbol{\beta}_{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{K_{XZ}}
satisfy
\|\boldsymbol{\Pi}\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{\infty}=O(1)
where
\boldsymbol{\Pi}
is defined in Proposition
S31
, and define
f_{n}\in\mathcal{S}^{d}_{r,N}
by
f_{n}(x,z):=\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\phi}(x,z)
. Further, define
g_{P,n}:[0,1]^{d_{Z}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
by
g_{P,n}(z):=\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigl(f_{n}(X,Z)\,|\,Z=z\bigr)
, let
\boldsymbol{\psi}
denote the
d_{Z}
-tensor B-spline basis of
\mathcal{S}_{2r-1,N}^{d_{Z}}
and let
\widetilde{K}_{Z}:=(2r-1+N)^{d_{Z}}
. Let
Y_{i}:=f_{n}(X_{i},Z_{i})
for
i\in[n]
, and let
\boldsymbol{\widehat{\theta}}
denote the ordinary least squares estimate from regressing
Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{n}
onto
\boldsymbol{\psi}(Z_{1}),\ldots,\boldsymbol{\psi}(Z_{n})
. If
\widetilde{K}_{Z}\log(\widetilde{K}_{Z})/n\to 0
, then

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigl(g_{P,n}(Z_{i})-\boldsymbol{\widehat{\theta}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\psi}(Z_{i})\bigr)^{2}=O_{\mathcal{P}}\bigl(\|\boldsymbol{\Pi}\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{\infty}^{2}\{\widetilde{K}_{Z}^{-2s/d_{Z}}+\widetilde{K}_{Z}/n\}\bigr).

Letting
\boldsymbol{\theta}_{P}\in\mathbb{R}^{\widetilde{K}_{Z}}
be the unique solution to
g_{P,n}^{\dagger}(z)=\boldsymbol{\theta}_{P}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\psi}(z)
, we have under the same assumptions that

\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{P}\|_{2}^{2}=O_{\mathcal{P}}\bigl(\|\boldsymbol{\Pi}\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{\infty}^{2}\widetilde{K}_{Z}^{2}/n\bigr).

Finally, if
(X^{*},Z^{*})
is a new observation of
(X,Z)
independent of the original sample, then

{\mathbb{E}}_{P}\Bigl(\bigl\{g_{P,n}(Z^{*})-\boldsymbol{\widehat{\theta}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z^{*})\bigr\}^{2}\bigm|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\Bigr)=O_{\mathcal{P}}\bigl(\|\boldsymbol{\Pi}\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{\infty}^{2}\{\widetilde{K}_{Z}^{-2s/d_{Z}}+\widetilde{K}_{Z}/n\}\bigr).

Proof.

We check the conditions of Proposition
S29
with
\mathcal{P}
in that result taken to be the set of distributions of
(Y_{1},Z_{1})
. To this end, let
\boldsymbol{\phi}^{Z}
and
\boldsymbol{\phi}^{X}
denote the
d_{Z}
- and
d_{X}
-tensor B-spline bases of
\mathcal{S}_{r,N}^{d_{Z}}
and
\mathcal{S}_{r,N}^{d_{X}}
, respectively, so that
\boldsymbol{\phi}(x,z)=\boldsymbol{\phi}^{X}(x)\otimes\boldsymbol{\phi}^{Z}(z)
. By Proposition
S31
, we can write

f_{n}(x,z)=\boldsymbol{\phi}(x,z)^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Pi}\boldsymbol{\beta}+\boldsymbol{\phi}^{Z}(z)^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{\beta}}.

Thus,

g_{P,n}(z)=\sum_{k=1}^{K_{Z}}\phi_{k}^{Z}(z)\biggl[\sum_{\ell=1}^{K_{X}}(\boldsymbol{\Pi}\boldsymbol{\beta})_{(k-1)K_{X}+\ell}{\mathbb{E}}(\phi^{X}_{\ell}(X)\,|\,Z=z)+\bar{\beta}_{k}\biggr]=:\sum_{k=1}^{K_{Z}}\phi_{k}^{Z}(z)h_{k}(z).

By Lemma
S32
and Assumption
(i)
, we have for every
k\in[K_{Z}]
that the function
r_{k}:[0,1]^{d_{Z}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
given by

r_{k}(z):=\sum_{\ell=1}^{K_{X}}(\boldsymbol{\Pi}\boldsymbol{\beta})_{(k-1)K_{X}+\ell}{\mathbb{E}}(\phi^{X}_{\ell}(X)\,|\,Z=z)

belongs to
\mathcal{H}_{s}^{d_{Z}}
with
\|r_{k}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{s}}\leq C\|\boldsymbol{\Pi}\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{\infty}
. Since the constant function
z\mapsto\bar{\beta}_{k}
belongs to
\mathcal{S}_{r,N}^{d_{Z}}
, we deduce from Proposition
S27
and Lemma
S33
that the
L_{2}(\mathcal{P})
-best approximant
g_{P,n}^{\dagger}
to
g_{P,n}
in
\mathcal{S}_{2r-1,N}^{d_{Z}}
satisfies for each
P\in\mathcal{P}
that

\displaystyle\|g_{P,n}-g_{P,n}^{\dagger}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq M(C,c,d_{Z},2r-1)\|g_{P,n}-g_{P,n}^{*}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{M(C,c,d_{Z},2r-1)C\|\boldsymbol{\Pi}\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{\infty}}{(2rK_{Z})^{s/d_{Z}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{d_{Z}}M(C,c,d_{Z},2r-1)C\|\boldsymbol{\Pi}\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{\infty}}{(2r\widetilde{K}_{Z})^{s/d_{Z}}}.

Thus, Assumption (i) of Proposition
S29
is satisfied with
\zeta=s/d_{Z}
. Assumption (ii) of Proposition
S29
is true by hypothesis, and Assumption (iii) of Proposition
S29
holds with
\sigma_{n}=\|\boldsymbol{\Pi}\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{\infty}
since

{\mathrm{Var}}(Y_{1}\,|\,Z_{1})={\mathrm{Var}}(f_{n}(X,Z)\,|\,Z)={\mathrm{Var}}(\boldsymbol{\phi}(X,Z)^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Pi}\boldsymbol{\beta}\,|\,Z)\leq\|\boldsymbol{\phi}(x,z)^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Pi}\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{\infty}^{2}\leq\|\boldsymbol{\Pi}\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{\infty}^{2}

by Proposition
S23
(b). The conclusions therefore follow from Proposition
S29
.
∎

Finally in this section, we present two results that control two different out-of-sample product errors in a sharper way than would be obtained via a naive application of the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality. The first can be regarded as a restated and uniform version of Theorem 8 and Lemma A5 in
Newey and Robins, 2018
.

Proposition S35
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a family of distributions of
(X,Y,Z)
on
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times[0,1]^{d}
with corresponding regression functions
f_{P},g_{P}:[0,1]^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
given by
f_{P}(z):={\mathbb{E}}_{P}(Y\,|\,Z=z)
and
g_{P}(z):={\mathbb{E}}_{P}(X\,|\,Z=z)
satisfying:

(i)

There exist
\zeta_{f}(d,r)\equiv\zeta_{f}>0
and
\zeta_{g}(d,r)\equiv\zeta_{g}>0
such that

\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\|f_{P}-f_{P}^{\dagger}\|_{\infty}=O(K^{-\zeta_{f}}),\quad\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\|g_{P}-g_{P}^{\dagger}\|_{\infty}=O(K^{-\zeta_{g}}),

where
f_{P}^{\dagger}
and
g_{P}^{\dagger}
denote the
L_{2}(P)
-best approximants of
f_{P}
and
g_{P}
respectively in
\mathcal{S}^{d}_{r,N}
.

(ii)

Each
P\in\mathcal{P}
is absolutely continuous with respect to Lebesgue measure on
[0,1]^{d}
, with corresponding density
p_{P}
satisfying
C:=\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\sup_{z\in[0,1]^{d}}p_{P}(z)<\infty
and
c:=\inf_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\inf_{z\in[0,1]^{d}}p_{P}(z)>0
.

(iii)

There exists a positive sequence
(\sigma_{n}^{2})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
such that
\max\bigl\{{\mathrm{Var}}(Y\,|\,Z),{\mathrm{Var}}(X\,|\,Z)\bigr\}\leq\sigma_{n}^{2}=O(1)
.

Now suppose we are given three independent samples
(X^{f}_{i},Y^{f}_{i},Z^{f}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
,
(X^{g}_{i},Y^{g}_{i},Z^{g}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
and
(X_{i},Y_{i},Z_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
, each consisting of
n
independent and identically distributed copies of
(X,Y,Z)
. Let
\boldsymbol{\phi}
denote the
d
-tensor B-spline basis of
\mathcal{S}^{d}_{r,N}
. Let
\boldsymbol{\widehat{\beta}}_{f}
and
\boldsymbol{\widehat{\beta}}_{g}
denote the ordinary least squares estimates from regressing
Y_{1}^{f},\ldots,Y_{n}^{f}
onto
\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{1}^{f}),\ldots,\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{n}^{f})
and
X_{1}^{g},\ldots,X_{n}^{g}
onto
\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{1}^{g}),\ldots,\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{n}^{g})
respectively. Define fitted regression functions
\widehat{f}
and
\widehat{g}
by
\widehat{f}(z)=\boldsymbol{\widehat{\beta}}_{f}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\phi}(z)
and
\widehat{g}(z)=\boldsymbol{\widehat{\beta}}_{g}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\phi}(z)
respectively. If
K\log(K)/n\to 0
, then

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigl\{\widehat{f}(Z_{i})-f_{P}(Z_{i})\bigr\}

\displaystyle\bigl\{\widehat{g}(Z_{i})-g_{P}(Z_{i})\bigr\}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathcal{P}}\biggl(K^{-(\zeta_{f}+\zeta_{g})}+\frac{K^{1/2}}{n}+\frac{K^{2-\max(\zeta_{f},\zeta_{g})}\log K}{n^{2}}\biggr).

Proof.

Suppose without loss of generality that
\zeta_{f}\geq\zeta_{g}
. Define
\boldsymbol{\beta}_{P,f},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{P,g}\in\mathbb{R}^{K}
so that
f_{P}^{\dagger}(z)=\boldsymbol{\beta}_{P,f}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\phi}(z)
and
g_{P}^{\dagger}(z)=\boldsymbol{\beta}_{P,g}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\phi}(z)
. We start with the decomposition

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigl\{\widehat{f}(Z_{i})-f_{P}(Z_{i})\bigr\}\bigl\{\widehat{g}(Z_{i})-g_{P}(Z_{i})\bigr\}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\{f_{P}^{\dagger}(Z_{i})-f_{P}(Z_{i})\}\{g_{P}^{\dagger}(Z_{i})-g_{P}(Z_{i})\}}_{\textup{I}_{n}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigl\{\widehat{f}(Z_{i})-f_{P}^{\dagger}(Z_{i})\bigr\}\bigl\{g_{P}^{\dagger}(Z_{i})-g_{P}(Z_{i})\bigr\}}_{\textup{II}_{n}^{f}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigl\{f_{P}^{\dagger}(Z_{i})-f_{P}(Z_{i})\bigr\}\bigl\{\widehat{g}(Z_{i})-g_{P}^{\dagger}(Z_{i})\bigr\}}_{\textup{II}_{n}^{g}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigl\{\widehat{f}(Z_{i})-f_{P}^{\dagger}(Z_{i})\bigr\}\bigl\{\widehat{g}(Z_{i})-g_{P}^{\dagger}(Z_{i})\bigr\}}_{\textup{III}_{n}}.

By Assumption
(i)
,

\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}|\textup{I}_{n}|\leq\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\|f_{P}-f_{P}^{\dagger}\|_{\infty}\|g_{P}-g_{P}^{\dagger}\|_{\infty}=O\bigl(K^{-(\zeta_{f}+\zeta_{g})}\bigr).

Using (
S47
) in the proof of Proposition
S29
(with
g_{P}
and
g_{P}^{\dagger}
in place of
f_{P}
and
f_{P}^{\dagger}
there), we deduce that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{P}\bigl((\textup{II}_{n}^{f})^{2}\,|\,\widehat{f}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\bigl(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\beta}}_{f}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{P,f}\bigr)^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{P}\bigl(\{g_{P}^{\dagger}(Z)-g_{P}(Z)\}^{2}\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z)\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z)^{\top}\bigr)\bigl(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\beta}}_{f}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{P,f}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\|g_{P}^{\dagger}-g_{P}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{P}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\beta}}_{f}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{P,f}\|_{2}^{2}=O_{\mathcal{P}}\bigl(K^{-(2\zeta_{g}-1)}n^{-2}\bigr),

by our hypothesis on
\|g_{P}^{\dagger}-g_{P}\|_{\infty}
, Proposition
S23
(d) and Proposition
S29
. Thus by Lemma
S2
,

\textup{II}_{n}^{f}=O_{\mathcal{P}}(K^{-(\zeta_{g}-1/2)}n^{-1})=O_{\mathcal{P}}(K^{1/2}n^{-1}).

Similarly,

\textup{II}_{n}^{g}=O_{\mathcal{P}}(K^{1/2}n^{-1}).

To deal with the
\textup{III}_{n}
term, define
\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}:=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{i})\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{i})^{\top}
, as well as
\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{f}:=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{i}^{f})\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{i}^{f})^{\top}
and
\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{g}:=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{i}^{g})\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{i}^{g})^{\top}
. For
i\in[n]
, let
\varepsilon_{i}^{f}:=Y_{i}^{f}-f_{P}(Z_{i}^{f})
,
\varepsilon_{i}^{g}:=X_{i}^{g}-g_{P}(Z_{i}^{g})
,
h_{i}^{f}:=f_{P}(Z_{i}^{f})-f_{P}^{\dagger}(Z_{i}^{f})
and
h_{i}^{g}:=g_{P}(Z_{i}^{g})-g_{P}^{\dagger}(Z_{i}^{g})
. We write

\displaystyle\textup{III}_{n}

\displaystyle=(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\beta}}_{f}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{P,f})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\beta}}_{g}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{P,g})

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{i}^{f}\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{i}^{f})^{\top}\biggr)\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{f}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\beta}}_{g}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{P,g})}_{\textup{III}_{n}^{(1)}}

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt+\underbrace{\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{i}^{f}\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{i}^{f})^{\top}\biggr)\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{f}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{g}^{-1}\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{i}^{g}\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{i}^{g})\biggr)}_{\textup{III}_{n}^{(2)}}

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt+\underbrace{\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{i}^{f}\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{i}^{f})^{\top}\biggr)\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{f}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{g}^{-1}\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{i}^{g}\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{i}^{g})\biggr)}_{\textup{III}_{n}^{(3)}}.

To deal with the
\textup{III}_{n}^{(1)}
term, we have using the fact that
{\mathbb{E}}_{P}(\varepsilon_{1}^{f}\,|\,Z_{1}^{f})=0
and
{\mathrm{Var}}_{P}(Y_{1}^{f}\,|\,Z_{1}^{f})={\mathbb{E}}\bigl((\varepsilon_{1}^{f})^{2}\,|\,Z_{1}^{f}\bigr)\leq\sigma_{n}^{2}
that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{P}\bigl((\textup{III}_{n}^{(1)})^{2}\,|\,\boldsymbol{\widehat{\beta}}_{g},(Z_{i},Z_{i}^{f})_{i=1}^{n}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sigma_{n}^{2}}{n}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\beta}}_{g}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{P,g})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{f}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{f}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{f}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\beta}}_{g}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{P,g})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sigma_{n}^{2}}{n}\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{f}^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\beta}}_{g}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{P,g}\|_{2}^{2}=O_{\mathcal{P}}(Kn^{-2}),

by our hypothesis on
\sigma_{n}^{2}
, (
S45
) in Lemma
S28
and Proposition
S29
. Hence, by another application of Lemma
S2
,

\textup{III}_{n}^{(1)}=O_{\mathcal{P}}(K^{1/2}n^{-1}).

To deal with the
\textup{III}_{n}^{(2)}
term, by the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality,

|\textup{III}_{n}^{(2)}|\leq\bigl\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{f}^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{g}^{-1/2}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}\biggl\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{f}^{-1/2}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{i}^{f}\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{i}^{f})\biggr\|_{2}\biggl\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{g}^{-1/2}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{i}^{g}\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{i}^{g})\biggr\|_{2}.

By (
S45
) in Lemma
S28
,

\bigl\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{f}^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{g}^{-1/2}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathcal{P}}(1).

The same argument as in (
S49
) in the proof of Proposition
S29
now yields that

\textup{III}_{n}^{(2)}=O_{\mathcal{P}}(K^{-(\zeta_{f}+\zeta_{g}-1)}n^{-1})=O_{\mathcal{P}}(K^{-(\zeta_{f}+\zeta_{g})}).

To deal with the
\textup{III}_{n}^{(3)}
term we write

\displaystyle\textup{III}_{n}^{(3)}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{i}^{f}\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{i}^{f})^{\top}\biggr)\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{P}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{g}^{-1}\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{i}^{g}\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{i}^{g})\biggr)}_{\textup{III}_{n}^{(3,1)}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{i}^{f}\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{i}^{f})^{\top}\biggr)(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{f}^{-1}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{P}^{-1})\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{P}^{-1}\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{i}^{g}\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{i}^{g})\biggr)}_{\textup{III}_{n}^{(3,2)}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{i}^{f}\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{i}^{f})^{\top}\biggr)(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{f}^{-1}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{P}^{-1})\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{g}^{-1}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{P}^{-1})\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{i}^{g}\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{i}^{g})\biggr)}_{\textup{III}_{n}^{(3,3)}}.

For the first term, we have by an argument similar to the
\textup{III}_{n}^{(1)}
term that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{P}\bigl((\textup{III}_{n}^{(3,1)})^{2}\,|\,(Z_{i},Z_{i}^{f},Z_{i}^{g})_{i=1}^{n}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sigma_{n}^{2}}{n}\biggl\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{i}^{f}\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{i}^{f})\biggr\|_{2}^{2}\bigl\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{P}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{g}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{g}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{g}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{P}^{-1}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}.

By Lemma
S2
, our assumption on
\sigma_{n}
, (
S29
) in the proof of Proposition
S29
, Proposition
S23
(d) and (
S45
) in the proof of Lemma
S28
, we therefore have

\textup{III}_{n}^{(3,1)}=O_{\mathcal{P}}(K^{1/2-\zeta_{f}}n^{-1})=O_{\mathcal{P}}(K^{1/2}n^{-1}).

Similarly,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{P}\bigl((\textup{III}_{n}^{(3,2)})^{2}\,|\,

\displaystyle(Z_{i},Z_{i}^{f},Z_{i}^{g})_{i=1}^{n}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sigma_{n}^{2}}{n}\biggl\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{i}^{f}\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{i}^{f})\biggr\|_{2}^{2}\bigl\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{P}^{-1}-\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{f}^{-1}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\bigl\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{P}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{g}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{P}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}.

Hence, by the same arguments as for
\textup{III}_{n}^{(3,1)}
, together with the second result in Lemma
S28
,

\textup{III}_{n}^{(3,2)}=O_{\mathcal{P}}\biggl(\frac{K^{-(\zeta_{f}-1)}\log^{1/2}(eK)}{n^{3/2}}\biggr)=O_{\mathcal{P}}(K^{1/2}n^{-1}).

Finally, by the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, we have

\displaystyle\bigl|\textup{III}_{n}^{(3,3)}\bigr|\leq\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{f}^{-1}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{P}^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\bigl\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{g}^{-1}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{P}^{-1}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}\biggl\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{i}^{g}\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{i}^{g})\biggr\|_{2}\biggl\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{i}^{f}\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{i}^{f})\biggr\|_{2},

so

\textup{III}_{n}^{(3,3)}=O_{\mathcal{P}}\biggl(\frac{K^{2-\zeta_{f}}\log(eK)}{n^{2}}\biggr)=O_{\mathcal{P}}\biggl(K^{-(\zeta_{f}+\zeta_{g})}+\frac{K^{2-\max(\zeta_{f},\zeta_{g})}\log K}{n^{2}}\biggr)

from our previous bounds. The result follows.
∎

Our second and final result controls a different type of product error and is loosely based on Theorem 8 and Corollary 9 in a working version of
Ichimura and Newey, 2015
.

Proposition S36
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a family of distributions of
(Y,Z)
on
\mathbb{R}\times[0,1]^{d}
with corresponding regression function
f_{P}:[0,1]^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
given by
f_{P}(z):={\mathbb{E}}_{P}(Y\,|\,Z=z)
. Further, let
(g_{P})_{P\in\mathcal{P}}
be a family of functions from
[0,1]^{d}
to
\mathbb{R}
with
\rho_{P}:={\mathbb{E}}_{P}\bigl(g_{P}(Z)^{2}\bigr)<\infty
and
\inf_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\rho_{P}>0
. Suppose that

(i)

There exist
\zeta_{f}(d,r)\equiv\zeta_{f}>0
and
\zeta_{g}(d,r)\equiv\zeta_{g}>0
such that

\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\|f_{P}-f_{P}^{\dagger}\|_{\infty}=O(K^{-\zeta_{f}}),\quad\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\|g_{P}-g_{P}^{\dagger}\|_{\infty}=O(K^{-\zeta_{g}}),

where
f_{P}^{\dagger}
and
g_{P}^{\dagger}
denote the
L_{2}(P)
-best approximants of
f_{P}
and
g_{P}
respectively in
\mathcal{S}^{d}_{r,N}
.

(ii)

Each
P\in\mathcal{P}
is absolutely continuous with respect to Lebesgue measure on
[0,1]^{d}
, with corresponding density
p_{P}
satisfying
C:=\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\sup_{z\in[0,1]^{d}}p_{P}(z)<\infty
and
c:=\inf_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\inf_{z\in[0,1]^{d}}p_{P}(z)>0
.

(iii)

There exists a positive sequence
(\sigma_{n}^{2})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
such that
{\mathrm{Var}}(Y\,|\,Z)\leq\sigma_{n}^{2}=O(1)
.

Now suppose we are given two independent samples
(Y^{f}_{i},Z^{f}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
and
(Y_{i},Z_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
, each consisting of
n
independent and identically distributed copies of
(Y,Z)
. Let
\boldsymbol{\phi}
denote the
d
-tensor B-spline basis of
\mathcal{S}^{d}_{r,N}
. Let
\boldsymbol{\widehat{\beta}}
denote the ordinary least squares estimate from regressing
Y_{1}^{f},\ldots,Y_{n}^{f}
onto
\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{1}^{f}),\ldots,\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{n}^{f})
. Define the fitted regression function
\widehat{f}
by
\widehat{f}(z)=\boldsymbol{\widehat{\beta}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\phi}(z)
. If
K\log(K)/n\to 0
, then

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{P}(Z_{i})

\displaystyle\bigl\{\widehat{f}(Z_{i})-f_{P}(Z_{i})\bigr\}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathcal{P}}\bigl(K^{-(\zeta_{f}+\zeta_{g})}+K^{-(\zeta_{g}-1/2)}n^{-1}+\rho_{P}^{1/2}n^{-1/2}\{1+K^{-(\zeta_{f}-1/2)}\}\bigr).

Proof.

Define
\boldsymbol{\beta}_{P,f},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{P,g}\in\mathbb{R}^{K}
so that
f_{P}^{\dagger}(z)=\boldsymbol{\beta}_{P,f}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\phi}(z)
and
g_{P}^{\dagger}(z)=\boldsymbol{\beta}_{P,g}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\phi}(z)
. By Proposition
S23
(b),

\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{P,g}\|_{2}\leq K^{1/2}c_{s}(r)^{-d}\{\|g_{P}^{\dagger}-g_{P}\|_{\infty}+\|g_{P}\|_{2}\}\leq K^{1/2}c_{s}(r)^{-d}\{\|g_{P}^{\dagger}-g_{P}\|_{\infty}+\rho_{P}^{1/2}c^{-1/2}\}.

Thus, by
(i)
,

\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{P,g}\|_{2}=O_{\mathcal{P}}(K^{-(\zeta_{g}-1/2)}+\rho_{P}^{1/2}K^{1/2}).

(S52)

We can write

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{P}(Z_{i})\bigl\{\widehat{f}(Z_{i})\!-\!f_{P}(Z_{i})\bigr\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\{g_{P}(Z_{i})\!-\!g_{P}^{\dagger}(Z_{i})\}\{f_{P}^{\dagger}(Z_{i})\!-\!f_{P}(Z_{i})\}}_{\textup{I}_{n}}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{P}^{\dagger}(Z_{i})\{f_{P}^{\dagger}(Z_{i})\!-\!f_{P}(Z_{i})\}}_{\textup{II}_{n}}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\underbrace{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\{g_{P}(Z_{i})-g_{P}^{\dagger}(Z_{i})\}\{\widehat{f}(Z_{i})-f_{P}^{\dagger}(Z_{i})\}}_{\textup{III}_{n}}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{P}^{\dagger}(Z_{i})\{\widehat{f}(Z_{i})-f_{P}^{\dagger}(Z_{i})\}}_{\textup{IV}_{n}}.

By assumption
(i)
again,

\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}|\textup{I}_{n}|\leq\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\|f_{P}-f_{P}^{\dagger}\|_{\infty}\|g_{P}-g_{P}^{\dagger}\|_{\infty}=O\bigl(K^{-(\zeta_{f}+\zeta_{g})}\bigr).

From (
S47
) in the proof of Proposition
S29
,

{\mathbb{E}}_{P}\bigl(g_{P}^{\dagger}(Z)\{f_{P}(Z)-f_{P}^{\dagger}(Z)\}\bigr)={\mathbb{E}}_{P}\bigl(\{f_{P}(Z)-f_{P}^{\dagger}(Z)\}\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z)^{\top}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{P,g}\bigr)=0,

and therefore

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{P}(\textup{II}_{n}^{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{P,g}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{P}\bigl(\{f_{P}(Z)-f_{P}^{\dagger}(Z)\}^{2}\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z)\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z)^{\top}\bigr)\boldsymbol{\beta}_{P,g}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\|f_{P}-f_{P}^{\dagger}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{P,g}\|_{2}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{P}\|_{\mathrm{op}}.

Thus

\textup{II}_{n}=O_{\mathcal{P}}\bigl(K^{-(\zeta_{f}-\zeta_{g})}n^{-1/2}+\rho_{P}^{1/2}K^{-\zeta_{f}}n^{-1/2}\bigr)=O_{\mathcal{P}}\bigl(K^{-\zeta_{f}-\zeta_{g}}+\rho_{P}^{1/2}n^{-1/2}\bigr).

The same argument as for the
\textup{II}_{n}^{f}
term in the proof of Proposition
S35
yields that

\textup{III}_{n}=O_{\mathcal{P}}\bigl(K^{-(\zeta_{g}-1/2)}n^{-1}\bigr).

To deal with
\textup{IV}_{n}
, we write, with quantities defined as in the proof of Proposition
S35
,

\displaystyle\textup{IV}_{n}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\beta}_{P,g}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{P,f})

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\boldsymbol{\beta}_{P,g}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{f}^{-1}\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{i}^{f}\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{i}^{f})\biggr)}_{\textup{IV}_{n}^{(1)}}+\underbrace{\boldsymbol{\beta}_{P,g}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{f}^{-1}\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{i}^{f}\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{i}^{f})\biggr)}_{\textup{IV}_{n}^{(2)}}.

Since
{\mathbb{E}}_{P}\bigl(\varepsilon_{1}^{f}\,|\,Z_{1}^{f}\bigr)=0
and
{\mathrm{Var}}_{P}\bigl(Y_{1}^{f}\,|\,Z_{1}^{f}\bigr)={\mathbb{E}}_{P}\bigl((\varepsilon_{1}^{f})^{2}\,|\,Z_{1}^{f}\bigr)\leq\sigma_{n}^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{P}\bigl((\textup{IV}_{n}^{(1)})^{2}\,|\,(Z_{i},Z_{i}^{f})_{i=1}^{n}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sigma_{n}^{2}}{n}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{P,g}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{f}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{f}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{f}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{P,g}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sigma_{n}^{2}}{n}\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{f}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{f}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{f}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{P,g}\|_{2}^{2},

so

\textup{IV}_{n}^{(1)}=O_{\mathcal{P}}\bigl((K^{-\zeta_{g}}+\rho_{P}^{1/2})n^{-1/2}\bigr)=O_{\mathcal{P}}(\rho_{P}^{1/2}n^{-1/2}).

Finally, by the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality,

\displaystyle|\textup{IV}_{n}^{(2)}|

\displaystyle\leq\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{P,g}\|_{2}\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}\boldsymbol{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{f}^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\biggl\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{i}^{f}\boldsymbol{\phi}(Z_{i}^{f})\biggr\|_{2}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathcal{P}}(K^{-(\zeta_{f}+\zeta_{g})+1/2}n^{-1/2}+\rho_{P}^{1/2}K^{-(\zeta_{f}-1/2)}n^{-1/2})

\displaystyle=O_{\mathcal{P}}\bigl(K^{-(\zeta_{f}+\zeta_{g})}+\rho_{P}^{1/2}K^{-(\zeta_{f}-1/2)}n^{-1/2}\bigr).

The result follows.
∎

Appendix S5
Univariate linear model analysis

In this section we give a more detailed analysis of the setting considered in Section
3.1
. In contrast to the remainder of this paper, we let
\mathcal{D}_{1}
contain
n_{1}
observations and
\mathcal{D}_{2}
contain
n_{2}
observations, and we let
2n=n_{1}+n_{2}
for this subsection only. All limiting statements in this section are interpreted as
\min\{n_{1},n_{2}\}\to 0
. This will facilitate a discussion of the effect of the splitting ratio on the power, and to compare the power of the proposed test more precisely with existing methods.
To simplify our analysis, we set
\widehat{v}\equiv 1
. We now formally write down the assumption required for the main result of this section (Proposition
S37
).

Assumption S1
.

Suppose that the family
\mathcal{P}
of joint distributions
P
of
(X,Y,Z)
satisfies the linear model (
8
). Let
\boldsymbol{\eta}_{P}
and
\boldsymbol{\theta}_{P}
denote the population least squares projections of
X
on
Z
and
Y
on
Z
, respectively. Let
\widehat{\beta}
,
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}}
and
\boldsymbol{\widehat{\theta}}
denote estimators of
\beta_{P}
,
\boldsymbol{\eta}_{P}
and
\boldsymbol{\theta}_{P}
, respectively, where
\widehat{\beta}
is trained on
\mathcal{D}_{2}
and
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}}
and
\boldsymbol{\widehat{\theta}}
are trained on
\mathcal{D}_{1}
. Assume that
\sigma_{\beta_{P}}^{2}:=\lim_{n_{2}\rightarrow\infty}\mathrm{Var}_{P}\bigl(\sqrt{n_{2}}(\widehat{\beta}-\beta_{P})\bigr)
exists in
(0,\infty)
, and suppose that
\widehat{\beta}
,
\boldsymbol{\widehat{\theta}}
and
\boldsymbol{\widehat{\eta}}
satisfy

\displaystyle\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\Big|{\mathbb{P}}_{P}\big(\sqrt{n_{2}}\sigma_{\beta_{P}}^{-1}(\widehat{\beta}-\beta_{P})\leq t\big)-\Phi(t)\Big|

\displaystyle=o(1),

(S53)

\displaystyle\sqrt{n_{1}}\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\theta}}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{P}\|_{2}\cdot\bigg\|\frac{1}{n_{1}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{1}}(X_{i}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{P}^{\top}Z_{i})Z_{i}\bigg\|_{2}

\displaystyle=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1),

(S54)

\displaystyle\sqrt{n_{1}}\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\eta}}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{P}\|_{2}\cdot\bigg\|\frac{1}{n_{1}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{1}}(Y_{i}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{P}^{\top}Z_{i})Z_{i}\bigg\|_{2}

\displaystyle=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1),

(S55)

\displaystyle\sqrt{n_{1}}\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\eta}}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{P}\|_{2}\cdot\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\theta}}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{P}\|_{2}\cdot\bigg\|\frac{1}{n_{1}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{1}}Z_{i}Z_{i}^{\top}\bigg\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1),

(S56)

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\theta}}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{P}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\frac{1}{n_{1}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{1}}(X_{i}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{P}^{\top}Z_{i})^{2}\|Z_{i}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1),

(S57)

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\eta}}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{P}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\frac{1}{n_{1}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{1}}(Y_{i}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{P}^{\top}Z_{i})^{2}\|Z_{i}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1),

(S58)

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\eta}}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{P}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\theta}}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{P}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\frac{1}{n_{1}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{1}}\|Z_{i}\|_{2}^{4}

\displaystyle=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1).

(S59)

In Section
3.1
we consider a simpler but less general assumption (Assumption
1
) that suffices for the analysis when the estimators are OLS estimators. These more general assumptions allow for settings where alternate estimators are used or the dimension is allowed to increase with
n
.

As mentioned before, we set the estimated weight function
\widehat{v}\equiv 1
in this analysis, which yields
L_{i}=\widehat{\beta}\bigl(Y_{i}-\boldsymbol{\widehat{\theta}}^{\top}Z_{i}\bigr)\bigl(X_{i}-\boldsymbol{\widehat{\eta}}^{\top}Z_{i}\bigr)=:\widehat{\beta}R_{i}
for
i=1,\ldots,n_{1}
. The resulting PCM statistic is

\displaystyle T=\mathrm{sgn}(\widehat{\beta})\frac{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{1}}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{1}}R_{i}}{\sqrt{\frac{1}{n_{1}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{1}}R_{i}^{2}-\bigl(\frac{1}{n_{1}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{1}}R_{i}\bigr)^{2}}}.

To simplify our presentation, we write,
\xi_{P}:=X-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{P}^{\top}Z
,
\varepsilon_{P}:=Y-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{P}^{\top}Z
,
\sigma_{P,\xi}^{2}:={\mathrm{Var}}_{P}(\xi_{P})
and
\sigma_{P,\varepsilon\xi}^{2}:={\mathrm{Var}}_{P}(\varepsilon_{P}\xi_{P})
. The following result provides asymptotic size and power expressions for the PCM test in this context.

Proposition S37
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{P}
is a family of distributions
P
of
(X,Y,Z)
for which the estimators
\widehat{\beta}
,
\boldsymbol{\widehat{\eta}}
and
\boldsymbol{\widehat{\theta}}
satisfy Assumption
S1
. In addition, assume that there exist
c,C,\delta>0
such that
\sigma_{P,\varepsilon\xi}^{2}>c
and
{\mathbb{E}}_{P}\bigl\{\bigl|\varepsilon_{P}\xi_{P}\bigr|^{2+\delta}\bigr\}\leq C
for all
P\in\mathcal{P}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Then, by letting

\displaystyle\psi_{P,\alpha,n}:=\Phi\biggl(\frac{\sqrt{n_{2}}\beta_{P}}{\sigma_{\beta_{P}}}\biggr)\Phi\biggl(z_{\alpha}+\frac{\sqrt{n_{1}}\beta_{P}\sigma_{P,\xi}^{2}}{\sigma_{P,\varepsilon\xi}}\biggr)+\Phi\biggl(-\frac{\sqrt{n_{2}}\beta_{P}}{\sigma_{\beta_{P}}}\biggr)\Phi\biggl(z_{\alpha}-\frac{\sqrt{n_{1}}\beta_{P}\sigma_{P,\xi}^{2}}{\sigma_{P,\varepsilon\xi}}\biggr),

the power of the PCM test satisfies

\displaystyle\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\big|{\mathbb{P}}_{P}(T>z_{1-\alpha})-\psi_{P,\alpha,n}\big|\rightarrow 0,\ \text{as $\min\{n_{1},n_{2}\}\rightarrow\infty$.}

Furthermore, when
\alpha<1/2
, we have
\psi_{P,\alpha,n}\geq\alpha
and, when
\sigma_{P,\xi}^{2}/\sigma_{P,\varepsilon\xi}>0
, equality holds if and only if
\beta_{P}=0
.

Proposition
S37
confirms that under Assumption
S1
and the given moment conditions, our proposed test is asymptotically valid uniformly over the null hypothesis
\mathcal{P}_{0}:=\{P\in\mathcal{P}:\beta_{P}=0\}
. In terms of splitting ratio, a consequence of Proposition
S37
, as stated formally in Corollary
S18
is that in this linear model setting one cannot hope to achieve high power against a local alternative where
\tau_{P}\asymp n^{-1}
unless
n_{1}\asymp n_{2}
.
While limited to the linear model, this result nevertheless instills confidence in our choice of balanced splitting ratio, and also reveals that the choice of splitting ratio that maximises the asymptotic power depends on the underlying (unknown) parameters. For this reason, we consider
n_{1}=n_{2}
by default for simplicity.

For the specific class of linear alternatives considered in Proposition
S37
, the asymptotic power of the GCM test
(
Shah and Peters, 2020
)
without sample splitting is

\displaystyle\Phi\Biggr(z_{\alpha/2}+\frac{\sqrt{n_{1}+n_{2}}\beta_{P}\sigma_{P,\xi}^{2}}{\sigma_{P,\varepsilon\xi}}\Biggr)+\Phi\Biggr(z_{\alpha/2}-\frac{\sqrt{n_{1}+n_{2}}\beta_{P}\sigma_{P,\xi}^{2}}{\sigma_{P,\varepsilon\xi}}\Biggr).

Comparing this expression with
\psi_{P,\alpha,n}
, one can see that the GCM test is typically more powerful than the proposed test, but only by a constant factor when
n_{1}\asymp n_{2}
. However, as mentioned earlier, the proposed test can have power against broader alternatives than the GCM test depending on the choice of projection. In comparison with the tests of
Williamson et al., 2023
and
Dai et al., 2024
, the proposed test achieves higher power. In particular, their tests become powerless whenever
\sqrt{n}\tau_{P}\rightarrow 0
, which is true for both parametric and nonparametric settings. Moreover, as pointed out by
Williamson et al., 2023
and further demonstrated in Section
S2.2
of the supplementary material, their tests via sample splitting may not control the Type I error when
(X,Y,Z)
are mutually independent. In contrast, our approach does not suffer from this issue and can be powerful even when
\sqrt{n}\tau_{P}\rightarrow 0
, as demonstrated by Proposition
S37
. In the next subsection, we provide the proof of Proposition
S37
.

S5.1
Proof of Proposition
S37

Throughout this proof we suppress
P
subscripts from the notation as in the proofs in Section
S1
. As
L_{i}=\widehat{\beta}\bigl(Y_{i}-\boldsymbol{\widehat{\theta}}^{\top}Z_{i}\bigr)\bigl(X_{i}-\boldsymbol{\widehat{\eta}}^{\top}Z_{i}\bigr)=:\widehat{\beta}R_{i}
for
i\in[n_{1}]
, recall that our test statistic is

\displaystyle T=\mathrm{sgn}(\widehat{\beta})\frac{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{1}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}R_{i}}{\sqrt{\frac{1}{n_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}R_{i}^{2}-\bigl(\frac{1}{n_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}R_{i}\bigr)^{2}}}.

(S60)

Let

\displaystyle T_{R}:=\frac{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{1}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}(R_{i}-\beta\sigma_{\xi}^{2})}{\sigma_{\varepsilon\xi}},

and for now suppose that the following approximations hold:

\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\big|{\mathbb{P}}\big(T_{R}\leq t\big)-\Phi(t)\big|=o(1)

(S61)

and

\Bigg\{\frac{\sigma_{\varepsilon\xi}^{2}}{\frac{1}{n_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}R_{i}^{2}-\big(\frac{1}{n_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}R_{i}\big)^{2}}\Bigg\}^{1/2}=1+o_{\mathcal{P}}(1).

(S62)

Then, by (
S62
), we have

\displaystyle T

\displaystyle=\biggl\{\mathrm{sgn}(\widehat{\beta})T_{R}+\mathrm{sgn}(\widehat{\beta})\frac{\sqrt{n_{1}}\beta\sigma_{\xi}^{2}}{\sigma_{\varepsilon\xi}}\biggr\}(1+V_{n}),

where
V_{n}
is a remainder term satisfying
V_{n}=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)
.
Define for brevity
s_{\beta}:=\sqrt{n_{1}}\beta\sigma_{\xi}^{2}/\sigma_{\varepsilon\xi}
. Now, since
(R_{i})_{i=1}^{n_{1}}
and
\mathrm{sgn}(\widehat{\beta})
are formed on independent data and are thus independent, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}|{\mathbb{P}}(T>z_{1-\alpha})-\psi_{\alpha,n}|

\displaystyle=\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\Bigl|{\mathbb{P}}\Bigl(\mathrm{sgn}(\widehat{\beta})T_{R}>z_{1-\alpha}-\mathrm{sgn}(\widehat{\beta})s_{\beta}-\mathrm{sgn}(\widehat{\beta})V_{n}(T_{R}+s_{\beta})\Bigr)-\psi_{\alpha,n}\Bigr|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\biggl|{\mathbb{P}}(\widehat{\beta}>0){\mathbb{P}}\Bigl(T_{R}>z_{1-\alpha}-s_{\beta}-V_{n}(T_{R}+s_{\beta})\Bigr)-\Phi\biggl(\frac{\sqrt{n_{2}}\beta}{\sigma_{\beta}}\biggr)\Phi\bigl(z_{\alpha}+s_{\beta}\bigr)\biggr|

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\biggl|{\mathbb{P}}(\widehat{\beta}<0){\mathbb{P}}\Bigl(-T_{R}>z_{1-\alpha}+s_{\beta}+V_{n}(T_{R}+s_{\beta})\Bigr)-\Phi\biggl(\frac{-\sqrt{n_{2}}\beta}{\sigma_{\beta}}\biggr)\Phi\bigl(z_{\alpha}-s_{\beta}\bigr)\biggr|

\displaystyle\hskip 327.20668pt+\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}{\mathbb{P}}(\widehat{\beta}=0).

The last term here is
o(1)
by (
S53
). The first two terms are dealt with similarly, so we only show how to argue that the first term is
o(1)
. To this end, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\biggl|{\mathbb{P}}(\widehat{\beta}>0){\mathbb{P}}\Bigl(T_{R}>z_{1-\alpha}-s_{\beta}

\displaystyle-V_{n}(T_{R}+s_{\beta})\Bigr)-\Phi\biggl(\frac{\sqrt{n_{2}}\beta}{\sigma_{\beta}}\biggr)\Phi\bigl(z_{\alpha}+s_{\beta}\bigr)\biggr|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\biggl|{\mathbb{P}}(\widehat{\beta}>0)-\Phi\biggl(\frac{\sqrt{n_{2}}\beta}{\sigma_{\beta}}\biggr)\biggr|

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\Bigl|{\mathbb{P}}\Bigl(T_{R}>z_{1-\alpha}-s_{\beta}-V_{n}(T_{R}+s_{\beta})\Bigr)-\Phi\bigl(z_{\alpha}+s_{\beta}\bigr)\Bigr|.

The first term above is
o(1)
by (
S53
). To deal with the second term, for an arbitrary
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, write

\displaystyle\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}

\displaystyle\Bigl|{\mathbb{P}}\Bigl(T_{R}>z_{1-\alpha}-s_{\beta}-V_{n}(T_{R}+s_{\beta})\Bigr)-\Phi\bigl(z_{\alpha}+s_{\beta}\bigr)\Bigr|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\Bigl|{\mathbb{P}}\Bigl(T_{R}>z_{1-\alpha}-s_{\beta}-V_{n}(T_{R}+s_{\beta}),\,|V_{n}T_{R}|<\epsilon,\,|V_{n}|<\epsilon\Bigr)-\Phi\bigl(z_{\alpha}+s_{\beta}\bigr)\Bigr|

\displaystyle\hskip 170.71652pt+\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}{\mathbb{P}}_{P}(|V_{n}|\geq\epsilon)+\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}{\mathbb{P}}_{P}(|V_{n}T_{R}|\geq\epsilon).

Since
V_{n}=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)
by (
S62
) and
V_{n}T_{R}=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)
by Lemma
S3
and (
S61
), the last two terms are
o(1)
. Moreover,

\displaystyle\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\Bigl|{\mathbb{P}}\Bigl(T_{R}>

\displaystyle z_{1-\alpha}-s_{\beta}-V_{n}(T_{R}+s_{\beta}),\,|V_{n}T_{R}|<\epsilon,\,|V_{n}|<\epsilon\Bigr)-\Phi\bigl(z_{\alpha}+s_{\beta}\bigr)\Bigr|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\max\Bigl\{{\mathbb{P}}\Bigl(T_{R}>z_{1-\alpha}-s_{\beta}-\epsilon(1+|s_{\beta}|)\Bigr)-\Phi\bigl(z_{\alpha}+s_{\beta}\bigr),

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\Phi\bigl(z_{\alpha}+s_{\beta}\bigr)-{\mathbb{P}}\Bigl(T_{R}>z_{1-\alpha}-s_{\beta}+\epsilon(1+|s_{\beta}|)\Bigr)\Bigr\}

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\Bigl|{\mathbb{P}}\Bigl(T_{R}>z_{1-\alpha}-s_{\beta}-\epsilon(1+|s_{\beta}|)\Bigr)-\Phi\bigl(z_{\alpha}+s_{\beta}\bigr)\Bigr|}_{\textup{I}_{n}}+

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\underbrace{\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\Bigl|\Phi\bigl(z_{\alpha}+s_{\beta}\bigr)-{\mathbb{P}}\Bigl(T_{R}>z_{1-\alpha}-s_{\beta}+\epsilon(1+|s_{\beta}|)\Bigr)\Bigr|}_{\textup{II}_{n}}.

We only show that
\textup{I}_{n}
is
o(1)
as
\textup{II}_{n}
can be handled similarly. Now letting
W\sim N(0,1)
, we have

\displaystyle\textup{I}_{n}

\displaystyle=\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\Bigl|{\mathbb{P}}\Bigl(T_{R}>z_{1-\alpha}-s_{\beta}-\epsilon(1+|s_{\beta}|)\Bigr)-{\mathbb{P}}\bigl(W>z_{1-\alpha}-s_{\beta}\bigr)\Bigr|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\Bigl|{\mathbb{P}}\Bigl(T_{R}>z_{1-\alpha}-s_{\beta}-\epsilon(1+|s_{\beta}|)\Bigr)-{\mathbb{P}}\bigl(W>z_{1-\alpha}-s_{\beta}-\epsilon(1+|s_{\beta}|)\bigr)\Bigr|

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt+\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\Bigl|{\mathbb{P}}\Bigl(W\in\bigl(z_{1-\alpha}-s_{\beta}-\epsilon(1+|s_{\beta}|),z_{1-\alpha}-s_{\beta}\bigr]\Bigr).

By the asymptotic normality of
T_{R}
in (
S61
), the first term is
o(1)
. On the other hand, for the second term

\displaystyle\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\Bigl|

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\Bigl(W\in\bigl(z_{1-\alpha}-s_{\beta}-\epsilon(1+|s_{\beta}|),z_{1-\alpha}-s_{\beta}\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\min\biggl\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}(1+|s_{\beta}|)\epsilon,\,\Phi\bigl(z_{1-\alpha}-s_{\beta}\bigr),\,\Phi\bigl(z_{\alpha}+s_{\beta}+\epsilon(1+|s_{\beta}|)\bigr)\biggr\}.

We now analyse the upper bound depending on the sign of
\beta
.

Case (i)
Suppose that
\beta>0
and
\Phi(z_{1-\alpha}-s_{\beta})\leq\epsilon/\sqrt{2\pi}
. Then

\displaystyle\min\biggl\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}(1+|s_{\beta}|)\epsilon,\,\Phi\bigl(z_{1-\alpha}-s_{\beta}\bigr),\,\Phi\bigl(z_{\alpha}+s_{\beta}+\epsilon(1+|s_{\beta}|)\bigr)\biggr\}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{2\pi}}.

On the other hand, if
\Phi(z_{1-\alpha}-s_{\beta})>\epsilon/\sqrt{2\pi}
, then
0<s_{\beta}<z_{1-\alpha}-\Phi^{-1}\bigl(\frac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\bigr)
. Thus

\displaystyle\min\biggl\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}(1+|s_{\beta}|)\epsilon,\,\Phi\bigl(z_{1-\alpha}-s_{\beta}\bigr)

\displaystyle,\,\Phi\bigl(z_{\alpha}+s_{\beta}+\epsilon(1+|s_{\beta}|)\bigr)\biggr\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\biggl\{1+z_{1-\alpha}-\Phi^{-1}\biggl(\frac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\biggr)\biggr\}.

Case (ii)
Next suppose that
\beta<0
and
\Phi\bigl(z_{\alpha}+s_{\beta}+\epsilon(1+|s_{\beta}|)\bigr)\leq\epsilon/\sqrt{2\pi}
. Then

\displaystyle\min\biggl\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}(1+|s_{\beta}|)\epsilon,\,\Phi\bigl(z_{1-\alpha}-s_{\beta}\bigr),\,\Phi\bigl(z_{\alpha}+s_{\beta}+\epsilon(1+|s_{\beta}|)\bigr)\biggr\}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{2\pi}}.

On the other hand, if
\Phi\bigl(z_{\alpha}+s_{\beta}+\epsilon(1+|s_{\beta}|)\bigr)>\epsilon/\sqrt{2\pi}
, then

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-\epsilon}\biggl\{\Phi^{-1}\biggl(\frac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\biggr)-z_{\alpha}-\epsilon\biggr\}<s_{\beta}<0.

Thus

\displaystyle\min\biggl\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}(1+|s_{\beta}|)\epsilon,\,\Phi\bigl(z_{1-\alpha}-s_{\beta}\bigr),\,

\displaystyle\Phi\bigl(z_{\alpha}+s_{\beta}+\epsilon(1+|s_{\beta}|)\bigr)\biggr\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\biggl[1+\frac{1}{1-\epsilon}\biggl\{z_{\alpha}+\epsilon-\Phi^{-1}\biggl(\frac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\biggr)\biggr\}\biggr].

Case (iii)
When
\beta=0
, we have

\displaystyle\min\biggl\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}(1+|s_{\beta}|)\epsilon,\,\Phi\bigl(z_{1-\alpha}-s_{\beta}\bigr),\,\Phi\bigl(z_{\alpha}+s_{\beta}+\epsilon(1+|s_{\beta}|)\bigr)\biggr\}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{2\pi}}.

Combining the previous results we have for every
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
that

\displaystyle\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}

\displaystyle\min\biggl\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}(1+|s_{\beta}|)\epsilon,\,\Phi\bigl(z_{1-\alpha}-s_{\beta}\bigr),\,\Phi\bigl(z_{\alpha}+s_{\beta}+\epsilon(1+|s_{\beta}|)\bigr)\biggr\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\max\biggl\{1,\,1+z_{1-\alpha}-\Phi^{-1}\biggl(\frac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\biggr),\,1+\frac{1}{1-\epsilon}\biggl[z_{\alpha}+\epsilon-\Phi^{-1}\biggl(\frac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\biggr)\biggr]\biggr\}.

We further note that the bound
\mathbb{P}(W\geq x)\leq(1/2)\cdot e^{-x^{2}/2}
for
x\geq 0
gives for
\epsilon<(1/2)\cdot\sqrt{2\pi}
that

\displaystyle-\epsilon\Phi^{-1}\biggl(\frac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\biggr)\leq\epsilon\sqrt{2\log\biggl(\frac{1}{2(1-\epsilon/\sqrt{2\pi})}}\biggr)\rightarrow 0,

as
\epsilon\rightarrow 0
. We deduce that
\textup{I}_{n}=o(1)
, so the first claim of the proposition will follow once we establish (
S61
) and (
S62
).

For the claim (
S61
), consider the decomposition

\displaystyle T_{R}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\frac{\sigma_{\varepsilon\xi}^{-1}}{\sqrt{n_{1}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}\bigl\{(Y_{i}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}Z_{i})(X_{i}-{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\top}Z_{i})-\beta\sigma_{\xi}^{2}\bigr\}}_{T^{(1)}}

\displaystyle-\underbrace{\frac{\sigma_{\varepsilon\xi}^{-1}}{\sqrt{n_{1}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}(Y_{i}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}Z_{i})({\boldsymbol{\widehat{\eta}}-\boldsymbol{\eta}})^{\top}Z_{i}}_{T^{(2)}}-\underbrace{\frac{\sigma_{\varepsilon\xi}^{-1}}{\sqrt{n_{1}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\theta}}-\boldsymbol{\theta})^{\top}Z_{i}(X_{i}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\top}Z_{i})}_{T^{(3)}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\frac{\sigma_{\varepsilon\xi}^{-1}}{\sqrt{n_{1}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\theta}}-\boldsymbol{\theta})^{\top}Z_{i}Z_{i}^{\top}({\boldsymbol{\widehat{\eta}}-\boldsymbol{\eta}})}_{T^{(4)}}.

By the assumption that
{\mathbb{E}}\bigl\{\bigl|(Y-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}Z)(X-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\top}Z)\bigr|^{2+\delta}\bigr\}\leq C
,
Shah and Peters, 2020
yields that

\displaystyle\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\big|{\mathbb{P}}\big(T^{(1)}\leq t\big)-\Phi(t)\big|\rightarrow 0.

Moreover,

|T^{(2)}|\leq c\sqrt{n_{1}}\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\eta}}-\boldsymbol{\eta}\|_{2}\biggl\|\frac{1}{n_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}(Y_{i}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}Z_{i})Z_{i}\biggr\|_{2}=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1),

by Cauchy–Schwarz, the assumption that
\sigma_{P,\varepsilon\xi}>c
and (
S55
). We can argue similarly that
T^{(3)}=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)
using (
S54
). Finally,

|T^{(4)}|\leq c\sqrt{n_{1}}\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\eta}}-\boldsymbol{\eta}\|_{2}\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\theta}}-\boldsymbol{\theta}\|_{2}\biggl\|\frac{1}{n_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}Z_{i}Z_{i}^{\top}\biggr\|_{\mathrm{op}}=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)

by similar arguments as above and (
S56
). Combining the above with the uniform version of Slutsky’s theorem, we have the desired claim (
S61
).

To prove (
S62
), we let
\widetilde{R}_{n,i}:=R_{n,i}-\beta\sigma_{\xi}^{2}
for
i\in[n_{1}]
and note that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}R_{i}^{2}-\biggl(\frac{1}{n_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}R_{i}\biggr)^{2}=\frac{1}{n_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}\widetilde{R}_{n,i}^{2}-\biggl(\frac{1}{n_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}\widetilde{R}_{n,i}\biggr)^{2}=\frac{1}{n_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}\widetilde{R}_{n,i}^{2}+o_{\mathcal{P}}(1),

where the second equality follows from the proof of (
S61
) above. To ease the notation further, for
i\in[n_{1}]
, we write

\displaystyle\widetilde{R}_{n,i}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{(Y_{i}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}Z_{i})(X_{i}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\top}Z_{i})-\beta\sigma_{\xi}^{2}}_{\textup{I}_{i}}-\underbrace{(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\theta}}-\boldsymbol{\theta})^{\top}Z_{i}(X_{i}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\top}Z_{i})}_{\textup{II}_{i}}

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt-\underbrace{(Y_{i}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}Z_{i})(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\eta}}-{\boldsymbol{\eta}})^{\top}Z_{i}}_{\textup{III}_{i}}+\underbrace{(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\theta}}-\boldsymbol{\theta})^{\top}Z_{i}Z_{i}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{\widehat{\eta}}-{\boldsymbol{\eta}})}_{\textup{IV}_{i}}.

Then

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}\widetilde{R}_{n,i}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}\textup{I}_{i}^{2}+\frac{1}{n_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}\textup{II}_{i}^{2}+\frac{1}{n_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}\textup{III}_{i}^{2}+\frac{1}{n_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}\textup{IV}_{i}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt-\frac{2}{n_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}\textup{I}_{i}\textup{II}_{i}-\frac{2}{n_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}\textup{I}_{i}\textup{III}_{i}+\frac{2}{n_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}\textup{I}_{i}\textup{IV}_{i}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\frac{2}{n_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}\textup{II}_{i}\textup{III}_{i}-\frac{2}{n_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}\textup{II}_{i}\textup{IV}_{i}-\frac{2}{n_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}\textup{III}_{i}\textup{IV}_{i}.

By the assumption that
{\mathbb{E}}\bigl\{\bigl|(Y-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}Z)(X-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\top}Z)\bigr|^{2+\delta}\bigr\}\leq C
,
Shah and Peters, 2020
yields that
\sigma_{\varepsilon\xi}^{-2}n_{1}^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}\textup{I}_{i}^{2}=1+o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)
. Moreover, by Cauchy–Schwarz,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}\textup{II}_{i}^{2}\leq\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\theta}}-\boldsymbol{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\frac{1}{n_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}(X_{i}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\top}Z_{i})^{2}\|Z_{i}\|_{2}^{2},

so (
S57
) together with
\sigma_{\varepsilon\xi}^{2}>c
implies that
\frac{\sigma_{\varepsilon\xi}^{-2}}{n_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}\textup{II}_{i}^{2}=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)
. Similarly,

\frac{\sigma_{\varepsilon\xi}^{-2}}{n_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}\textup{III}_{i}^{2}=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)

by (
S58
). By two applications of Cauchy–Schwarz, we have

\frac{1}{n_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}\textup{IV}_{i}^{2}\leq\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\theta}}-\boldsymbol{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\widehat{\eta}}-\boldsymbol{\eta}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\frac{1}{n_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}\|Z_{i}\|_{2}^{4},

so (
S59
) combined with the lower bound on
\sigma_{\varepsilon\xi}^{2}
yields that
\frac{\sigma_{\varepsilon\xi}^{-2}}{n_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}\textup{IV}_{i}^{2}=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)
. Turning to the cross-product terms, by Cauchy–Schwarz and the previous analysis,

\displaystyle\biggl|\frac{\sigma_{\varepsilon\xi}^{-2}}{n_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}\textup{I}_{i}\textup{II}_{i}\biggr|\leq\sigma_{\varepsilon\xi}^{-2}\biggl(\frac{1}{n_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}\textup{I}_{i}^{2}\biggr)^{1/2}\biggl(\frac{1}{n_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}\textup{II}_{i}^{2}\biggr)^{1/2}=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1).

The other terms can be similarly analysed and shown to be
o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)
. We have thus established by the uniform version of Slutsky’s theorem that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sigma_{\varepsilon\xi}^{2}}\biggl\{\frac{1}{n_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}R_{i}^{2}-\biggl(\frac{1}{n_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}R_{i}\biggr)^{2}\biggr\}=1+o_{\mathcal{P}}(1).

Finally, (
S62
) follows by the above result combined with Lemma
S7
. This completes the proof of the first claim in Proposition
S37
.

To prove the second claim, let us assume that
\beta\geq 0
(the case
\beta<0
can be handled very similarly), and denote

\displaystyle\psi_{\alpha,n}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\Phi\biggl(\frac{\sqrt{n_{2}}\beta}{\sigma_{\beta}}\biggr)}_{V(\beta)}\cdot\underbrace{\Phi\biggl(z_{\alpha}+\frac{\sqrt{n_{1}}\beta\sigma_{\xi}^{2}}{\sigma_{\varepsilon\xi}}\biggr)}_{W_{1}(\beta)}+\underbrace{\Phi\biggl(-\frac{\sqrt{n_{2}}\beta}{\sigma_{\beta}}\biggr)}_{1-V(\beta)}\cdot\underbrace{\Phi\biggl(z_{\alpha}-\frac{\sqrt{n_{1}}\beta\sigma_{\xi}^{2}}{\sigma_{\varepsilon\xi}}\biggr)}_{W_{2}(\beta)}

\displaystyle=W_{2}(\beta)+V(\beta)\{W_{1}(\beta)-W_{2}(\beta)\}.

Then
V(\beta)\geq 1/2
and
W_{1}(\beta)-W_{2}(\beta)\geq 0
, so
\psi_{\alpha,n}\geq W_{2}(\beta)+\{W_{1}(\beta)-W_{2}(\beta)\}/2=W_{1}(\beta)/2+W_{2}(\beta)/2
.

Next observe that the function
\delta\mapsto\Phi(z_{\alpha}+\delta)/2+\Phi(z_{\alpha}-\delta)/2
is continuous on
\mathbb{R}
, and when
\alpha<1/2
, it is decreasing when
\delta<0
and increasing when
\delta>0
. It follows that

\displaystyle\psi_{\alpha,n}\geq\frac{1}{2}\Phi\biggl(z_{\alpha}+\frac{\sqrt{n_{1}}\beta\sigma_{\xi}^{2}}{\sigma_{\varepsilon\xi}}\biggr)+\frac{1}{2}\Phi\biggl(z_{\alpha}-\frac{\sqrt{n_{1}}\beta\sigma_{\xi}^{2}}{\sigma_{\varepsilon\xi}}\biggr)\geq\alpha,

and when
\sigma_{\xi}^{2}/\sigma_{\varepsilon\xi}>0
, we have equality in both inequalities if and only if
\beta=0
.

Appendix S6
Additional simulations: GAMs with binary responses

Here we consider settings similar to those considered in Section
6.1
, but with
Y
binary. Our null settings use

{\mathbb{P}}(Y=1\,|\,X,Z)=\mathrm{expit}(\sin(2\pi Z_{1})),

and we consider three alternative settings mirroring those in Section
6.1
:

1.

{\mathbb{P}}(Y=1\,|\,X,Z)=\mathrm{expit}(\sin(2\pi Z_{1})+0.25X^{2})
,

2.

{\mathbb{P}}(Y=1\,|\,X,Z)=\mathrm{expit}(\sin(2\pi Z_{1})+0.5X^{2})
,

3.

{\mathbb{P}}(Y=1\,|\,X,Z)=\mathrm{expit}(\sin(2\pi Z_{1})+0.5X^{2}Z_{2})
.

For all regressions with binary responses, we fit a binomial generalised additive model with logistic link, and we use additive models for all other regressions; we use the implementations in the R package
mgcv

(
Wood, 2017
)
. We do not fit a new regression model for
\widetilde{v}
but instead utilize that
Y
is binary and set
\widetilde{v}(x,z):=\widehat{g}(x,z)(1-\widehat{g}(x,z))
. The additive models are tuned as in Section
6.1
while the binomial additive models use half as many basis functions i.e.
\lfloor(N-1)/(2d)\rfloor
(where
N
and
d
are the number of observations and predictors on which the model is trained, respectively) to avoid issues with convergence of the generalised additive model fits.
The results can be seen in Figure
S2
and are broadly in line with those in Section
6.1
with the PCM performing favourably though being powerless in Setting 3 with pure interactions (as to be expected), and
wgsc
and most notably
gam
not maintaining Type I error control.

Figure S2:
Rejection rates in the various settings considered in Section
S6
for nominal 5%-level tests.
